# Mosshatch plan

Last updated 2026-09-29. Companion files: `docs/DECISIONS.md` (one entry per decision) and `docs/research/` (the evidence: one dossier per topic plus the notes from the independent verifier who tried to refute it).

## Status

| Phase | State | Notes |
|---|---|---|
| 0 Plan | **Complete, waiting for your "go"** | Research, plan, decisions. No application code exists |
| 1 The place (no backend) | Not started | Starts after "go" and the answers to the open decisions that touch it |
| 2 Accounts and money | Not started | Blocked on: registrar, fee, payment path, entity and counsel decisions |

| 4 The Nest | Not started | |

## What Phase 0 found, in one page

1. **Registrar.** Use **OpenSRS (Tucows)** first: the only candidate whose published contract is written for resale, with wholesale prices published for all six extensions and a sandbox. Fallback **CentralNic Reseller**. Details and the full comparison are in 4.1. The main costs are operational: an XML API, an IP allow-list, no idempotency key, replace-all DNS, and a registrant-facing interface that must be switched off before the first live registration.
2. **Price.** The USD 1.50 placeholder fails. Recommended, in three levels (D-003, which departs from the brief's single flat fee): USD 4.00 per domain-year where wholesale is under USD 50 a year, USD 9.00 from USD 50 to under USD 100 (`.studio`, `.io`) and USD 10.00 from USD 100 (`.ai`), the same on registration, renewal and transfer-in. First orders at November 2026 wholesale come to `.com` USD 19.25, `.dev` 21.00, `.app` 25.00, `.studio` 60.00, `.io` 69.00 and `.ai` 242.00 for the mandatory two years (121.00 a year). `.com` at USD 19.25 compares with about USD 11 at retail registrars: Mosshatch will not be the cheapest, and the plan says so plainly (4.2). Infrastructure costs about USD 201 to 204 a month plus Private Data Transfer on the recommended path (Vercel Static IPs), which breaks even at 81 to 82 domain-years a month at USD 2.50 per domain-year, or about USD 87 to 90 a month (35 to 36 domain-years) with a self-run gateway; Neon, gateway and monitoring figures are our own estimates.
3. **Payments.** Register while the money is only authorized, then capture. Cancelling an authorization is free; a refund keeps Stripe's fee. This departs from the brief's `payment_intent.succeeded` gate (D-002) and carries one open legal question. RAA 3.7.4 lets a credit-card charge (or equivalent) suffice "provided that the obligation to pay becomes final and non-revocable by the Registered Name Holder upon activation"; the duty is the accredited registrar's (Tucows), which is paid from Mosshatch's prepaid OpenSRS balance. Whether it flows down to Mosshatch, and whether an authorization hold meets the proviso, are questions for counsel. If not, the fallback is capture-then-refund at a cost of about USD 0.90 per failed registration.
4. **`.ai` and `.io` need care.** `.ai` has a two-year minimum (USD 222 wholesale at first charge, USD 242 with our fee), no privacy service, public contacts, and is absent from the OpenSRS sandbox. `.io`'s future is uncertain and it also has no privacy service. Both are built as creatures from day one but sold only after tests and disclosures (D-006).
5. **Stack.** Confirmed with changes: Vercel **Pro** is required (Hobby forbids commercial use), with three projects (`web`, `registrar`, `cards`); Drizzle's `neon-http` driver cannot do transactions, so use `node-postgres`; session cookies must be `SameSite=Lax` (Strict breaks the return from Stripe); Node 24; TypeScript 7 through an alias; AWS KMS for the vault (4.3a). Vercel Static IPs are one address pair per region shared with a small group of other customers, so the OpenSRS allow-list narrows a leaked key but is not a second factor (D-005).
6. **Security.** A written threat model with 46 rows and 155 named security tests (4.6), counted from the table, each due in one phase (section 5). Two secret read paths, for you to veto (D-019): a step-up per web reveal, and bearer bindings created by a passkey that read without a per-read ceremony. Honest limit: the vault is not zero-knowledge; a full compromise of the production `web` project exposes what it can decrypt until the key is denied. The vault and step-up code get a paid independent review before Phase 4 ends, and there is an external penetration test before launch (D-032).
7. **Compliance.** ICANN's new transfer rules (the TAC, no lock after registrant change) were adopted by the Board on 2026-06-07 but have **no effective date**; build to today's policy. Launch United States only until tax registrations exist. A checklist of 73 obligations (`docs/COMPLIANCE.md`, summarised in 4.7) has a phase against every row, and each phase's exit lists the rows it must build, with the questions for counsel.
8. **Names.** No blocking trademark conflict found for Mosshatch or Hatchkind; CROSSHATCH (Class 42) is the closest collision and HATCH is crowded, so a professional clearance is needed. `hatchkind.com` as the public card host is workable under strict conditions (4.8). Custody of both names matters more than their creation date, because `mosshatch.com` is the passkey RP ID (D-033, threat row 28).
9. **Feasibility of the scene.** A procedural WebGL2 scene fits the budgets: the measured lazily loaded scene chunk is 134 kB gzip, first paint can be real HTML, and deterministic screenshot tests work in this container. What cannot be verified here (real GPUs, Safari, thermals) is listed in 4.10.
10. **Research quality.** 37 research dossiers; 30 of them were followed by 39 independent verification passes, each by an agent instructed to refute the load-bearing claims by re-fetching the primary sources. The 3 measurement dossiers (bundle size, Playwright and WebGL, secret scan) and the 4 reference-site studies were single-analyst by design. The verification digest (`docs/research/verification-digest.md`) records 247 claims as corrected, refuted or unverifiable (for example: the Namecheap `.studio` price basis; the ERRP start date; the `.ai` two-year minimum date; Porkbun's API quotes; the Transfer Policy status). Corrections are applied in the text; where a verifier could not settle a point the sentence says so. The search tool's 200-call budget ran out early, so most later work used direct fetches; sites that blocked access were not worked around and the affected claims are marked unverified.

## Assumptions (veto any of these)

**Business and legal**
1. Mosshatch contracts as a company or sole proprietor (upstream contracts and Stripe need a legal identity), and a lawyer and an accountant are engaged (D-007).
2. Launch is United States only, USD only, cards and wallets only; no ACH, no Link until its capture window is documented (D-006).
3. The customer is always the registrant of record; Mosshatch never registers a name in its own name for someone else (D-020).
4. No promotional first-year prices at launch; "renews at" is read from the registrar's renewal price, and if register and renew prices differ both are shown at the same size (D-003).
5. Registrar-side auto-renew is off; Mosshatch drives renewal; a name never silently rolls over (D-008).
6. "WHOIS privacy: Free" means default redaction under the Registration Data Policy for the gTLDs; `.ai` and `.io` disclose what they expose (D-020).
7. Both domains you named (`mosshatch.com`, `hatchkind.com`) are yours: public RDAP shows only the registrar and the creation time (2026-09-29 17:48 UTC), and neither can leave the current registrar before about 2026-11-28.

**Technology**
8. Node 24.x, npm workspaces, Vite 8, React 19, three 0.186, TypeScript 7 via the alias; versions are re-verified at the start of Phase 1 (D-013).
9. One region: Vercel `iad1`, Neon `aws-us-east-1`, AWS KMS us-east-1. A Neon region cannot be changed later (D-014).
10. Vercel Pro from the first deployment (Hobby is non-commercial); Neon Launch until real customer money, then Scale (D-014).
11. Session idle 15 minutes and absolute 8 hours; step-up challenges live 120 seconds; the recovery design in 4.5 (D-016).
12. Strict CSP without nonces; Trusted Types in Report-Only first; no service worker in Phase 1 (D-015).
13. Fonts: Young Serif 400 and Atkinson Hyperlegible Next (variable, weights 400 to 700), self-hosted and subset, both SIL OFL 1.1 (measured 38.8 kB total).
14. At most 24 individually animated creatures in the Grove; more render as instanced silhouettes and appear in the Ledger (D-018).
15. The audit chain head is anchored daily to a store outside the database; the mechanism is chosen in Phase 2. Card snapshots are small PNGs stored outside the main database; the store is chosen in Phase 6.
16. A `?debug=states` route ships only in non-production builds.

**Process**
17. Commits stay local on `claude/vigilant-ritchie-8vinxb`; no push or pull request until you ask. The container is ephemeral, so ask for a push whenever you want the docs kept elsewhere (D-022).
18. Your brief is not committed to the repository unless you want it there.
19. Where a real value is unknown in the prototype (price, date, status), the UI shows a labelled placeholder ("sample price"); the product calls the adapter (brief section 0).

**Security, operations and content defaults (each is a Proposed default in `docs/DECISIONS.md`; veto any of them)**
20. Sensitive DNS: a change by an agent or a recipe to MX, NS, DS, apex A/AAAA/CNAME, SPF/DMARC TXT and the wider classes in 4.3b needs a human passkey (`dns.sensitive.approve`), and one approval binds a whole recipe plan. Your own edits in a signed-in session are not gated (a stated residual risk; closing it costs a passkey on every such edit) (D-011, D-034).
21. Availability search fans out to the six extensions on every settled query; Mosshatch never registers, reserves or sells a searched name and stores no search text beyond the cache (D-017).
22. Agent surface: two secret read paths (a step-up per web reveal; a bearer binding created by a passkey that reads `dev` and `preview` without a per-read ceremony, with `prod` only when named); static bearer tokens first, OAuth in Phase 5; agent proposals expire after 72 hours, bindings default to 30 days and at most 90; no stored-card charging for agent purchases (D-019).
23. Testing: Playwright `~1.63.0`, deterministic screenshots, and a manual real-device frame-time protocol; no frame time is claimed from this container (D-021).
24. Four deployment environments (local, preview, staging, production) with a mode guard; previews use fixture login, no vault and no registrar credentials; staging has its own hostname and RP ID (D-025).
25. Loss radius: multi-Region keys with a replica, separate production, staging and log-archive AWS accounts, and a break-glass role on hardware keys; the Terms say the Nest is not a backup of record (D-026).
26. Backup targets (own targets): recovery point 5 minutes, recovery time 4 hours for reads and 8 for writes, nightly encrypted off-Neon backups (D-027).
27. Operations: you are the sole on-call with a second person holding the emergency sheet; S1 and S2 alerts with own-target thresholds (N = 3 DoH samples 30 seconds apart, alert above 5 reveals in an hour, staleness bound 15 minutes); auto-safe mode after 30 minutes unacknowledged (D-028).
28. Account closure: a pseudonymous audit chain, an expiring export, erasure by key destruction, and erased data may persist in database history for the restore window (D-030).
29. Registry-premium names are not sold at launch; a price guard refuses any quote that is premium or differs from the extension's standard price (D-031).
30. A paid independent review of the vault and step-up code before Phase 4 ends, an external penetration test before launch, and a disclosure policy with `security.txt` (D-032).
31. `mosshatch.com` and `hatchkind.com` are renewed for at least five years with auto-renew on, in an account with the strongest two-factor sign-in the registrar offers and a recovery email on neither domain (D-033).
32. Public cards are `noindex` until the owner opts in (D-035).
33. Sound is off by default with one header toggle; rarity is cosmetic; the Arrival demo is labelled "Demo", plays once and is cancelled by any input (D-036).
34. Transfer copy never says "instant" or gives a fixed cap or a typical duration (D-037).
35. The quote expiry is never shown as a countdown; chip copy says renewal prices can change and you are emailed 21 days before any charge (D-038).
36. Beyond 24 domains the Grove shows the 24 that renew soonest as full creatures; every other domain is a focusable button in a list beside the Grove and a row in the Ledger; distant silhouettes are decoration only (D-018).
37. Renewal cadence: the charge is 10 days before expiry, notices go out at E-43, E-32, E-18 and E+1 (the ICANN expiry notices are sent regardless), and a price-change reminder is emailed 21 days before the charge (D-008).

## 4. Phase 0 findings

Each subsection answers one of the eight deliverables in the brief's Phase 0. Numbers carry their source in the text or in `docs/research/`; anything unverified says so.
### 4.1 Registrar comparison (sources re-fetched 2026-09-29; no live API call)

**How this was done.** Nine providers got a full dossier (resale terms, all thirteen `RegistrarAdapter` methods, sandbox, rate limits, wholesale prices for the six launch extensions, lifecycle and transfer behaviour), and each dossier was then attacked by independent verifiers who re-fetched the primary sources. Nine more routes were screened. No account exists, so no registrar API was called: every API verdict is a documentation verdict, and the "Live-verification status" row says what each column rests on. The verifiers changed the outcome in several places (for example Porkbun's API quotes `.io` and `.studio` at their renewal prices, not the promotional prices on its website; the Namecheap `.studio` price basis was wrong; Dynadot has a REST v1 that is not labelled beta). Corrections are applied in the text; where a verifier could not settle a point, the sentence says so. Evidence is in `docs/research/reg-*.md` and `docs/research/verification-digest.md`.

**Recommendation.**
- **Primary: OpenSRS (Tucows Domains Inc., IANA 69), Essential tier.** It is the only one of the researched candidates that combines a contract written for resale ("wishes to use, resell and/or provision the Tucows Services for itself and the benefit its own Users", MSA preamble), the end customer as registrant of record, no ICANN accreditation for Mosshatch, published wholesale prices for all six extensions and a sandbox (Horizon). eNom, from the same company, is the same offer. Signup is documented as automated and self-serve (a sales gate cannot be excluded: the API page ends "Speak with our sales team to get started"). Terms: a USD 95 one-time activation fee that converts to account credit (prepaid wholesale, not a running cost), a prepaid USD balance, no monthly fee and no advertised minimums (MSA 12.8 reserves the right to require them). Sources, accessed 2026-09-29: https://assets.opensrs.com/Uploads/Master-Services-Agreement.pdf ; https://opensrs.com/payment-terms ; https://opensrs.com/join ; https://opensrs.com/api/overview ; https://support.opensrs.com/support/solutions/articles/201000063061 (Horizon).
- **Fallback: CentralNic Reseller (Key-Systems GmbH, IANA 269), for new registrations and per-extension routing only.** It has an explicit Wholesale MSA plus Reseller Schedule, a modern REST API (OpenAPI, 287 operations, live in production since 2026-07-28), an OT&E sandbox and an optional IP allow-list (off by default, so it would not need Static IPs). But only `.com` is publicly priced (USD 18.33 at Basic Plus, 13.82 at Elite Plus), it wants a EUR 75 prepay, and its reseller duties are heavy. Before it counts as a real fallback: (1) get written confirmation or a waiver of the stated qualification of 100 registered or transferred domains within a year (the FAQ says "must", the pricing page "should"); (2) have counsel accept or negotiate Reseller Schedule 3.6 (Mosshatch is liable for all fees whether or not its customers pay) and MSA 13.7 (every domain must be transferred out before the end of the term); (3) note the sign-up attestation that the account is for a legally established business or for portfolios held outside the EU, not for private individuals in the EU, with immediate termination otherwise: the US-only launch (D-006) meets it, but get CentralNic's written permission before admitting EU individuals. One setting to change, not paper to clear: the account default renewal mode is AutoRenew and debits the prepaid balance, so set it to match D-008 (registrar-side auto-renew off) before the first registration. It is not a failover: an existing domain can leave OpenSRS only by ICANN inter-registrar transfer with each registrant's code and approval. If the registrar is down or in maintenance, orders wait with the payment authorized and the customer reads "Registrations are paused while our registrar is in maintenance. Nothing was charged." (states and retries are in 4.3b). If CentralNic's written quote for the other five extensions is worse than Dynadot's list prices, Dynadot becomes the fallback once its ToU s3.1(d) conflict is resolved in writing. Sources, accessed 2026-09-29: https://www.centralnicreseller.com/wholesale-services-agreements/ ; https://www.centralnicreseller.com/faqs/ ; https://www.centralnicreseller.com/domain-reseller-pricing/ ; https://www.centralnicreseller.com/sign-up/ ; https://kb.centralnicreseller.com/how-to/building-your-integration/rest-api-integration-guide/ ; https://kb.centralnicreseller.com/domains/renewal-system/.
- **Cost benchmark, not a fallback yet: Dynadot / Global Domain Group (IANA 3956).** Free, application-gated Reseller Program and the lowest list prices among the providers researched in depth (`.com` 10.88; GoDaddy's API Reseller wholesale lists `.com` at 10.69, but its terms are unread). The paper is one-sided (Dynadot ToU s3.1(d) requires that its terms prevail over the reseller's own customer agreement, which conflicts with Mosshatch owning its customer contract), REST v2 is beta-labelled (REST v1, 103 commands, is not), and `.dev` and `.app` are missing from its own list of API-supported TLDs. Worth a written clarification and a sandbox test before Phase 3, because per-extension routing to a cheaper provider is possible through the adapter. Sources, accessed 2026-09-29: https://www.dynadot.com/domain/prices ; https://www.dynadot.com/terms-of-use ; https://www.dynadot.com/domain/api-document ; https://www.dynadot.com/help/question/API-supported.
- **Not chosen:** Porkbun (best API ergonomics, but no reseller program and its API document says it "does not establish a reseller relationship"; no API for lock, auth code or transfer-out; the USD 100 per-order cap refuses `.ai` in its sandbox), Namecheap (no program, binding terms unread, no wholesale tier, no auth-code or transfer-out API), Gandi (no wholesale list: a new reseller pays retail, `.com` renews at USD 38.38), Name.com (retail cost basis; its reseller indemnity is uncapped, as OpenSRS 22.1 also is), NameSilo (reseller terms only visible after login), ResellerClub (its own site and prices were unreachable), AWS Route 53 Domains (no sandbox, default quota of 20 domains), GoDaddy (operative reseller terms unreadable), Cloudflare and Vercel (terms bar resale). Sources, accessed 2026-09-29: https://porkbun.com/api/json/v3/spec ; https://api.gandi.net/docs/domains/ ; https://docs.name.com/api/v1/overview ; https://aws.amazon.com/route53/domain-registration-agreement/ ; https://developer.godaddy.com/en/docs/api-users/domains ; https://developers.cloudflare.com/registrar/registrar-api/ ; https://vercel.com/legal/domain-name-registration-and-services-terms.

**Comparison (prices in USD per year; register equals renew unless noted; promotional first-year prices excluded because they would break "renews the same"). Method-by-method coverage is in 4.1a below.**

| | OpenSRS Essential | CentralNic Reseller | Dynadot (reseller) | Porkbun | Namecheap (retail register price; renewals are higher) |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| Resale permitted | Yes, explicit (MSA) | Yes, explicit (MSA 5.2, Reseller Schedule) | Yes, explicit Reseller Program; one-sided paper | Conditional; no program, duties fall on the account holder | Support pages say yes; binding ToS unread |
| Joining | USD 95, credited; prepaid | EUR 75 prepay; signed schedules; stated 100-domain qualification | Free, application (often instant) | Account; prepaid credit | Balance / spend gate; IPv4 allow-list |
| API | XML over HTTPS, port 55443, MD5 signature | REST (OpenAPI) plus legacy HTTPS | REST v2 (beta-labelled) and REST v1 (103 commands) | REST JSON v3.44 | XML |
| Adapter gaps | One lookup per call; DNS through `SET_DNS_ZONE` (no CAA, no TTL; the docs do not say whether it replaces the whole zone, so read-modify-write under a lock); `SW_REGISTER` defaults `period` to 2, so always send it; no idempotency key (reconcile with `GET_ORDERS_BY_DOMAIN`); outbound transfer approved by emailed link, not API; cannot redeem `.app`, `.ai` or `.io` through the API | No webhooks, no idempotency key; 1 command per second; `status[]` not documented as EPP codes | DNS has no record IDs and defaults to replace-all; no idempotency key; `.dev`/`.app` not in API TLD list | No API for lock, auth code, transfer-out; USD 100 order cap | No auth code, no transfer-out, replace-all DNS, no webhooks, no idempotency |
| Sandbox | Horizon; USD 5,000 test credit; no allow-list needed; cannot simulate transfers or redemption; `.ai` absent | OT&E; same operations, different behavior; bi-weekly maintenance | `api-sandbox.dynadot.com`; webhooks not testable | Free; keys need no account; transfers complete instantly | Separate account |
| Egress | Live IP allow-list: five rules by default (more on request to Support), /25 to /32 | Optional allow-list, off by default: up to five IPv4 addresses or ranges (/24 to /30) | Optional for reseller accounts on REST since 2025-10-09; CIDR supported, changes take 10 minutes | Per-key IP scoping optional | IPv4 allow-list required |
| Webhooks | HMAC-signed plus poll | None (poll `GET /event`) | 11 events, no retry policy documented | Signed webhooks in sandbox | None |
| Rate limits | Unpublished; lookups "may be rate-limited ... excessive use may result in a fee" | 1 command per second | 60 requests per minute, 1 thread; bulk search 5 names, 1,000 per day | 1 create per second; 25-name bulk check | Sources conflict: 20 or 50 per minute |
| Add grace and refund | Length and refund rule not published (`REVOKE` refunds only inside the grace period "as defined by each registry"). Storefront KB only: revoke within 5 days, USD 1 fee. Question 3 | Deletes inside the add grace period return `PAYBACK`; a per-zone quota exists (sample 9%, 5 days). Exact terms unverified; MSA 14.8 says fees are not refundable except as stated | Grace delete within 5 days (118 hours); refund is account credit less a per-extension fee, rate-capped at 10%, success not guaranteed; new accounts cannot grace delete | 4 days (96 hours; the DNRA says 5 days); website only, no API; 5% refund fee; some extensions excluded | No API; registrations are final; discretionary refund only if contacted within 5 days (120 hours) |
| Expiry grace, redemption, auction | Expiry grace 40 days, redemption 30 days (all six). Nameservers switch to Tucows 3 days after expiry (one KB article says on the expiry date). Live auction from day 41 to 45; a queued name cannot be redeemed | Renewal date is 35 days after expiry for gTLDs, but R&M 1.7 says no renewal 30 days after expiry and the Registration Agreement says an unpaid registrant loses rights 3 days before expiry: the timelines conflict, so promise no date. Redemption 30 days, then 5 days pending delete. Provider may park or auction from 5 days (R&M) or 14 days (Registration Agreement) after expiry; `.ai` deleted 43 days after expiry | Renewal grace 30 days, restore 30 days. Sources conflict: a help example shows a 40-day redemption, and ToU s8 gives a 40-day post-expiration period then 30 days. ToU s8.4 lets Dynadot auction names not renewed in the first 30 days, with no reseller carve-out | Renew at the normal price; normal function for 10 days, then parking or hold; auction from about day 21; removed on day 35 to 45. Redemption 30 days via Support only | About 30 days to renew a gTLD at the regular rate, then possible auction. Redemption 30 days via Support only, fee set upstream and unpublished. Release about 70 to 85 days |
| Transfer-in duration | Docs disagree: minutes to about 2 weeks (owner approval 5 or 7 days, review 24 to 48 hours, losing registrar 5 days, up to 9 more days before a declined one is cancelled). Not testable in Horizon | gTLDs finalise automatically 5 days after the losing registrar's email if unanswered; `.ai` without a code up to 7 days; charged at initiation | "1-15 days" (Dynadot); losing registrar approves by default after 5 days | "5-7 days" (API document), "5 to 7 business days" (KB); sandbox completes instantly | Confirmed automatically within 5 days of submission, plus 24 to 48 hours at the registry; up to 5 to 7 days awaiting release |
| Transfer-out and locks | No API to start or approve; the registrant approves by emailed link and silence for 5 days approves; the 60-day lock returns error 552; `.io` codes come via support | Gaining registrar initiates; approve or deny by API or `transfermode`; CentralNic re-applies its lock 30 days after unlock; `.io` and `.studio` have a 60-day registry lock | Auth-code endpoint is synchronous and returns 409 during the 60-day lock, when expired or expiring, or under UDRP; transfer-away order approved or denied by API; no transfer-out in the last 10 days of the post-expiration period | No API for lock, auth code or transfer-out; 60-day contractual lock | No auth-code or transfer-out API |
| Restore through the API | `REDEEM` for `.com`, `.dev` and `.studio` only, not `.app`, `.ai` or `.io`; fee from the rate card only (`.com` 80, `.dev` 150, `.app` 150, `.studio` 80, `.io` 250, `.ai` 200); not testable in Horizon | Restore is billed separately (`.com` 98.88); a restore command is not documented in the dossier (unverified) | `POST /domains/{domain}/restore` (`.com` 100.92 to `.ai` 460.00) | No: website and Support only (USD 200) | No: Support only |

| `.dev` | 17 | not public | 12.50 (12.84 register on the reseller tab, unexplained) | 12.87 | 10.98 retail |
| `.io` | 60 | not public | 53.50 | 51.80 (API quote) | 34.98 retail |
| `.app` | 21 | not public | 14.50 (14.98 register on the reseller tab, unexplained) | 14.93 | 10.98 retail |
| `.studio` | 42 (51 from 2026-10-06) | not public | 33.39 | 32.44 (API quote) | 39.98 (KB, rising to 51.48 on 2026-10-06) |

Question 15 | Default redaction per its KB; paid add-on exists; the `.ai` page lists no privacy service; not verified | Free, on by default | Free | Free, but API default is off (`WGEnabled=No`) |

ResellerClub is not tabulated: its own site and prices were unreachable (`docs/research/reg-resellerclub.md`, section 0). Namecheap prices other than `.studio` are third-party scrapes because its own pages were blocked. Dynadot cells show renewal prices, and the cost model in 4.2 uses them. Sources, accessed 2026-09-29: OpenSRS https://opensrs.com/domains/pricing and https://opensrs.com/domains/tld-price-changes ; CentralNic https://www.centralnicreseller.com/domain-reseller-pricing/ ; Dynadot https://www.dynadot.com/domain/prices and https://www.dynadot.com/domain/grace-deletion ; Porkbun https://api.porkbun.com/api/json/v3/pricing/get and https://porkbun.com/api/json/v3/spec ; Namecheap https://domainoffer.net/tld/studio/namecheap and https://tldes.com/studio (third-party) and its knowledge base article 10835. Lifecycle cells: https://support.opensrs.com/support/solutions/articles/201000063315 ; https://support.opensrs.com/support/solutions/articles/201000114626 (Storefront refunds) ; https://kb.centralnicreseller.com/how-to/working-with-domains/complete-domain-lifecycle/ ; https://www.dynadot.com/help/question/renew-domain ; https://kb.porkbun.com/article/37-what-happens-after-a-domain-expires ; https://kb.porkbun.com/article/293-can-domain-names-be-changed-or-refunded ; https://www.namecheap.com/support/knowledgebase/article.aspx/9916/2207/tlds-grace-periods/.

OpenSRS volume tiers (annual spend across domains, email and SSL, and registrations or transfers): Advanced USD 2,000 and 100 takes `.com` to 13.50 (14.25 from 2026-11-01); Premium USD 50,000 and 500 to 12.50 (13.25); Enterprise USD 100,000 and 1,000 to 11.50 (12.25). The timing conflicts: the rate card says a tier is honored to the end of the calendar year and then reassessed, while the knowledge base says tiers are reviewed each January and take effect on 1 April. The lowest available price always applies and promotions do not stack. Sources, accessed 2026-09-29: https://opensrs.com/domains/pricing ; https://support.opensrs.com/support/solutions/articles/201000063182.

**Other routes screened** (`docs/research/reg-screen-others.md`): eNom (Tucows) is the same wholesale price table with a USD 195 enrollment fee (shown as USD 50 on the day) and no benefit over OpenSRS; AWS Route 53 Domains explicitly treats a party registering domains for third parties as an ICANN reseller (agreement s3.12) and supports all six extensions, but has no sandbox, a default quota of 20 domains and no wholesale discount (`.com` 16, `.ai` 137 with a 2-year minimum and no privacy); GoDaddy has an OTE sandbox and a reseller plan sold to "select businesses", but its API terms and Reseller Agreement returned 403, so the resale clauses are unread; Cloudflare's registration API is beta with no renewals or transfers and its agreement bars selling access; Vercel's terms ban resale; Squarespace's reseller program is approval-gated with partner-gated docs; Hover has no public API (resale runs through OpenSRS or eNom); Spaceship and Epik could not be evaluated (bot protection or egress policy); Internet.bs's test API and price list were read (`.io` 49.98, `.ai` 92.40, `.studio` 31.42, `.dev` 12.17, `.app` 14.78, `.com` 11.30; wholesale or retail unknown) but its terms were blocked. Becoming an accredited registrar instead costs USD 3,500 to apply and USD 4,000 a year, plus roughly USD 70,000 of evidenced liquid capital, data escrow, RDAP and separate onboarding with each registry: a long-run option only. Sources, accessed 2026-09-29: https://www.icann.org/en/contracted-parties/accredited-registrars/how-to-become-a-registrar ; https://aws.amazon.com/route53/pricing/ ; https://reseller.squarespace.com/ ; https://www.hover.com/resell-domains ; https://testapi.internet.bs/.

**What the OpenSRS account means for the product.**
- **Price changes.** Appendix A promises at least 30 days' notice, but Tucows may pass through ICANN and registry fee changes (MSA 12.6) and change premium fees "without notice". Two changes were already scheduled on the day of research: `.studio` +USD 9 on 2026-10-06 and `.com` +USD 0.75 on 2026-11-01; when they were first posted is unknown. A third-party tracker also lists a +12.0% `.io` registry step on 2027-01-19 (unverified), and the `.com` contract allows up to +7% a year from 2027-10-26. The flat-fee model must absorb the timing gap; the customer notice policy is stated once, in 4.2.
- **Risk allocation and termination.** Tucows has broad suspension and termination rights, including under 22.2: if Tucows is threatened with suit and Mosshatch does not give written assurance of its indemnity, the account may be suspended or terminated. An ICANN-policy violation can end the agreement immediately (3.9), and the agreement cannot be assigned without consent (23.4), which matters for any future sale or financing. The allocation is one-way: Tucows excludes lost revenue, profit, goodwill, data and consequential loss (21.5) and limits the customer's sole remedy to termination and return of any unused deposit (21.6), while Mosshatch indemnifies Tucows with no stated monetary cap for claims about its products, fees, billing and customer service, its user agreements, and trademark or copyright disputes over names registered through it (22.1; see also 3.10 and 3.16). Tucows also reserves the right to require minimum order levels (12.8) and to charge USD 35 on any chargeback or disputed payment amount (12.10). Counsel must read the MSA before signing.
- **Expiry.** Unrenewed names can be auctioned by Tucows around day 41 to 45 (MSA 9.1), and a queued name cannot be redeemed by the registrant. Mosshatch renews before expiry. Parked pages with pay-per-click advertising (MSA Section 8) can be opted out of, generally or per domain (8.5): Mosshatch should opt out. Whether a reseller can opt out of the auction is written question 7; if it cannot, treat E+40 as a hard deadline. The auction and nameserver-switch dates are Tucows policy, not registry data, and the dossier's sources disagree on the switch date, so they come from a per-registrar policy profile and are labelled as such.
- **Balance.** A balance below zero is a material breach (MSA 12.1) and a depleted balance blocks all services (MSA 12.7). The deposit is non-refundable on account closure (KB) although MSA 21.6 mentions return of unused deposit. Hold the float defined under "Working capital" in 4.2 and alert on `GET_BALANCE`.
- **Static egress (D-005).** The live API needs an allow-listed IP: five rules by default, more on request to Support. Vercel Static IPs cost USD 100 per project per month on Pro plus Private Data Transfer (from USD 0.15 per GB, for all outbound traffic of that project including builds), so they go on the `registrar` project only, which stays small and carries no other traffic (three-project topology in 4.3b). The address pair per region is shared with a small group of other Vercel customers, so the allow-list narrows where a stolen key can be used from but is not a second factor. Static IPs apply to every environment of the project, Preview included, so only the production deployment may hold the live key. Horizon needs no allow-list, so the decision is needed before the live rehearsal, not before Phases 1 to 3. The stronger option is a self-run gateway on a dedicated address that holds the registrar credentials so they are never in Vercel; Vercel Secure Compute (Enterprise) is another. Recommendation: Static IPs for simplicity, with the gateway named as the stronger option. Sources, accessed 2026-09-29: https://vercel.com/docs/networking/static-ips ; https://vercel.com/pricing ; https://support.opensrs.com/support/solutions/articles/201000063475 ; https://support.opensrs.com/support/solutions/articles/201000063062.
- **A second control plane.** Each `SW_REGISTER` creates a registrant profile (`reg_username`, `reg_password`) that can sign in to the OpenSRS Manage Web Interface, where the registrant sees and changes the auth code, and password resets and transfer-approval links go to the registrant mailbox. The interface and auth-code visibility can be switched off at reseller-account level (KB 201000063287; untested). Until that is confirmed, "step-up before code release" is not the only route to a code (the controls are in 4.3b and 4.6). Source, accessed 2026-09-29: https://support.opensrs.com/support/solutions/articles/201000063287.
- **Not verifiable without an account:** real rate limits and the lookup fee, the add-grace-period length and refund rule per extension, whether `SET_DNS_ZONE` replaces the whole zone, what happens on a duplicate `SW_REGISTER`, the `.ai` first charge, and `.ai` and `.io` behaviour (`.ai` is not in Horizon). Horizon cannot show these either, so they are closed only by a live test: the rehearsal before public launch (section 5, Phase 6). These are the written questions to ask before paying the activation fee:
1. What are the lookup rate limits, and what is the "excessive use" fee (MSA 3.2 and 12.11)? Showing eggs needs up to six lookups per settled search. Can a daily ceiling be agreed in writing?
2. How many allow-list rules will Support add beyond five, and is there any way to call the live API without an allow-listed address?
3. What are the add-grace-period length and refund rule for each of the six extensions, and what is the reseller's monthly cap under the AGP Limits Policy?
4. What does a duplicate or retried `SW_REGISTER` return, and is there an order lookup by our own reference?
5. `.ai` and `.io`: which contacts appear publicly, how are transfers and auth codes handled, and can `.ai` be tested before going live?
6. Who sends the ICANN-required registrant verification and expiry emails for reseller domains, in whose branding and on what schedule (OpenSRS documents 90, 60, 30 and 5 days before and 3 and 10 days after expiry)? Mosshatch must avoid duplicates and must not miss the mandatory ones.
7. Can a reseller opt out of the expired-domain auction (from day 41 to 45), and what are the exact dates of expiry grace, nameserver switch and release?
8. When were the `.studio` (+USD 9, 2026-10-06) and `.com` (+USD 0.75, 2026-11-01) increases first notified, and is the 30-day notice in Appendix A honored per change?
9. Which data-processing addendum applies today (the published one dates from 2018 and refers to Privacy Shield)?
10. How is a change of registrant handled (Designated Agent default, contact-privacy exemption, the 60-day lock), and does it match the February 2024 Transfer Policy?
11. Is there an approve, decline or cancel call or event for an outbound transfer, who sends the Losing FOA, and how are `.io` codes requested? Can a reseller place a registrar hold (a transfer-away precondition is "no hold status")? Do not ask to suppress OpenSRS's email unless an ICANN-template Losing FOA still reaches the registrant within 24 hours.
12. Volume-tier timing (calendar year on the rate card, 1 April in the knowledge base) and whether promotions apply to API orders.
13. Deposit terms: the knowledge base says the deposit is non-refundable on closure while MSA 21.6 mentions return of unused deposit.
14. Will Tucows authorize ACH for a new API reseller with a US bank account, what are the per-payment ceiling and the three-day request cap, and is wire the accepted alternative? What are the card top-up limits?
15. For gTLD registrations by US registrants, do you redact registrant name, street, postal code, phone, email and organization in both RDAP and WHOIS by default, at no charge and without `f_whois_privacy=1` or the USD 3 add-on? Will you commit to that in writing and give notice before changing it?
16. Is there any manual review, sales step or KYC before API access is enabled?
17. Can the Manage Web Interface (including the transfer-approval and registrant-verification pages) be disabled for a reseller, and does "hide auth codes" cover `.io`? What does support require before issuing an `.io` code or changing contacts, IP rules or the API key, and does the Reseller Control Panel offer MFA or hardware keys?
18. Is DNSSEC key material free through the API or USD 500 per transaction (the MDRA and the API guide disagree)?

#### 4.1a Adapter coverage

The thirteen `RegistrarAdapter` methods against the three candidates that have an API and a sandbox. Each cell reads verdict (yes, partial or no), then the command or endpoint, then the caveat. Verdicts are the dossiers' own; none is "no", and every one rests on documentation, not on a live call. Restore, DNSSEC and the other additions listed in 4.3b sit outside these thirteen; restore is a row of the comparison table above. Sources, accessed 2026-09-29: OpenSRS https://domains.opensrs.guide/docs/quickstart (each command has its own page under https://domains.opensrs.guide/docs/); CentralNic https://rest-ote.rrpproxy.net/v1/openapi.json , https://kb.centralnicreseller.com/api/api-command/modifydnszone/ and https://kb.centralnicreseller.com/api/api-command/queryforeigntransferlist/ ; Dynadot https://www.dynadot.com/domain/api-document.

| Method | OpenSRS | CentralNic | Dynadot |
|---|---|---|---|
| `checkAvailability` | **Partial.** `LOOKUP DOMAIN`, one name per call; `NAME_SUGGEST`; `SUBMIT BULK_CHANGE` availability check returns a CSV by email. Reads the OpenSRS cache unless `no_cache=1`; taken is `response_code` 211 with `is_success=1`; premium names look taken unless the premium tier is enabled; MSA 3.2 lookup fee unquantified | **Yes.** `GET /domain/-/availability?domain=...` (CheckDomains). Up to 32 names per call, so the client builds the name x extension product; premium looks "not available" until premium handling is enabled; about 1 command per second | **Partial.** `GET /domains/bulk_search`; single `GET /domains/{domain}/search`. 5 names per request on a regular account, 1,000 bulk searches a day; results can come from cache (`confidence`); `register` is the final authority (409) |
| `quote` | **Yes.** `GET_PRICE DOMAIN` with `reg_type` new, renewal, transfer or trade, `all_periods=1`. Includes the ICANN fee; `is_registry_premium` only if the tier is enabled; no restore type, so the restore fee comes only from the rate card | **Yes.** `GET /domain/{domain}/price?type=ADDDOMAIN` or `RENEWDOMAIN`, `TRANSFERDOMAIN`, `RESTOREDOMAIN`. Premium class in `X-FEE-CLASS`; account currency defaults to EUR; only `.com` is publicly priced | **Partial.** Search with `show_price=true`; catalogue `GET /domains/get_tld_price`. Price is for the calling account's level; premium needs `register_premium: true`; REST `renew` has no `price_check` |
| `register` | **Yes.** `SW_REGISTER DOMAIN` `reg_type=new`. `period` defaults to 2 if omitted, so always send it, with `handle=process` and `auto_renew=0` (account defaults can bill the reseller balance); needs `reg_username` and `reg_password`; no idempotency key; unfunded or taken orders are "forced to the pending queue" | **Yes.** `POST /contact` then `POST /domain` (AddDomain). Two steps; `.app` and `.dev` need `x-accept-ssl-requirement=1`; premium needs a fee handshake; no idempotency key, and a duplicate is expected to return 554 (untested) | **Yes.** `POST /domains/{domain}/register`. Response has no order id; one domain per call; 402 when unfunded; 500 "order created" is indeterminate; `.ai` needs 2 years; no idempotency key |
| `renew` | **Yes.** `RENEW DOMAIN` with `currentexpirationyear` and `period`. A retry after success fails with 555, 465 or 541, a natural guard; renewals are final; a failed renewal leaves a draft that must be cleared | **Yes.** `PATCH /domain/{domain}/renew` with `period` and `expiration`. `expiration` guards double renewal (555); CentralNic works to its own renewal date, 35 days after expiry for gTLDs | **Yes.** `POST /domains/{domain}/renew` with `year`. A `year` mismatch returns 400, which guards a retry; 409 "Order exists already" |
| `getDomain` | **Partial.** `GET DOMAIN` `type=all_info` plus `type=status`, `whois_privacy_state`, `GET_REGISTRANT_VERIFICATION_STATUS`. No raw EPP status strings; the lock state needs the second call; returns nothing once a domain is deleted in redemption (use `GET_DELETED_DOMAINS`) | **Yes.** `GET /domain/{domain}` (StatusDomain). Read both `status[]` and `x-registry-status[]`, neither documented as EPP codes; `.ai` and `.io` status may be thin; use `renewal_date` for deadlines | **Partial.** `GET /domains/{domain}`. No EPP status array; Dynadot's own status vocabulary; redemption and pending delete cannot be told apart |
| `setLock` | **Yes.** `MODIFY DOMAIN` `data=status`, `lock_state` 0 or 1. `can_modify=0` if OpenSRS holds an internal lock; locking available for all six extensions | **Yes.** `PATCH /domain/{domain}` `transferlock=0` or `1`. Not supported by every registry; CentralNic re-applies the lock 30 days after unlock; registry lock is a separate paid service | **Yes.** `PUT /domains/{domain}/domain_lock`. Unlocking needs "api skip lock agreement" switched on in the control panel; 409 during auction, buy or installment locks |
| `listRecords` | **Partial.** `GET_DNS_ZONE`. Only with SystemDNS nameservers; A, AAAA, CNAME, MX, SRV, TXT; no TTL, no CAA | **Yes.** `GET /dnszone/{dnszone}/rr`. Provider-hosted KeyDNS; record lines, not IDs; the zone must exist | **Partial.** `GET /domains/{domain}/records`. No record IDs; one TTL for the whole domain; absent if the domain is not on Dynadot DNS |
| `upsertRecord` | **Partial.** `SET_DNS_ZONE` after `CREATE_DNS_ZONE`. No per-record operation; the docs do not say whether it replaces the whole zone or only the types supplied, so read-modify-write under a lock and test in Horizon. The control-panel guide limits TXT text to 254 characters and the API limit is undocumented, so a 2048-bit DKIM key may not fit | **Partial.** `PATCH /dnszone/{dnszone}` with `addrr[]`, `delrr[]`, `rr[]`. No native upsert (a repoint is delete plus add in one command); delete needs the exact line; TTL floor 60; only effective on KeyDNS nameservers | **Partial.** `POST /domains/{domain}/records`. Default replaces all records; `add_dns_to_current_setting: true` appends; no in-place update |
| `deleteRecord` | **Partial.** Same `SET_DNS_ZONE`; `DELETE_DNS_ZONE` deletes the entire zone | **Yes.** Same `PATCH` with `delrr[]` or `delallrr`. The exact record line is needed | **Partial.** `DELETE /domains/{domain}/records`, matched by type and value, not by ID |
| `startTransferIn` | **Yes.** `SW_REGISTER` `reg_type=transfer` with `auth_info`; pre-check `CHECK_TRANSFER`. Funds for the extra year are held (`.ai` adds 2); not testable in Horizon; one KB article says only the reseller web interface can supply the code with the order, while the API lists `auth_info` | **Yes.** `POST /domain/{domain}/transfer` `action=REQUEST` with `auth`; pre-check `GET .../transfer/availability` for some extensions only. Charged at initiation; owner contact mandatory for `.com` and `.studio`; `.ai` adds 2 years | **Yes.** `POST /domains/{domain}/transfer_in`. `duration` must be 1; empty response body, so look up the order with `GET /orders`; fee paid up front |
| `getTransfer` | **Yes.** `CHECK_TRANSFER check_status=1`; `GET_TRANSFERS_IN`; event `transfer.status_change`. Only transfers Mosshatch initiated; the docs give 5 and 7 days for the owner step | **Partial (poll only).** `GET /domain/{domain}/transfer`; `GET /event?class=DOMAIN_TRANSFER`. No webhook | **Yes.** `GET /domains/{domain}/transfer_status`; webhooks `order_completed`, `domain_status_changed`. The v2 status vocabulary is undocumented; webhooks cannot be tested in the sandbox |
| `getAuthCode` | **Yes (partial for `.io`).** `GET DOMAIN` `type=domain_auth_info`; `SEND_AUTHCODE`. OpenSRS support sets `.io` codes; the `SEND_AUTHCODE` recipient is unclear after the Registration Data Policy | **Yes (gTLD via StatusDomain).** `GET /domain/{domain}` property `auth`. Not verified live for `.ai` and `.io`; ICANN's 5-day release rule applies | **Yes.** `GET /domains/{domain}/transfer_auth_code`. Synchronous; 409 during the 60-day lock, when expired or expiring, or under UDRP; the skip-lock requirement is documented for the legacy API only |
| `startTransferOut` | **Partial.** None; observe with `GET_TRANSFERS_AWAY` (status filter `pending_admin`, `pending_owner`, `pending_registry`; no event documented, so poll). The gaining registrar starts it; the owner approves by emailed link and silence for 5 days approves; no approve or decline API | **Partial (composite).** Unlock with `PATCH transferlock=0`; list with `GET /domain/-/foreigntransfer`; `POST /domain/{domain}/transfer` `action=APPROVE` or `DENY`; policy via `PUT .../transfermode`. Default gTLD path is the FOA email, finalised after 5 days | **Partial.** None to initiate; `getAuthCode` plus `setLock(false)`; `POST /orders/{order_id}/authorize_transfer_away`; webhook `domain_transfer_away`. ICANN's 5-day default approval applies; Dynadot's own timing is unverified |

Gap that changes the product: through the OpenSRS API, `restore()` is unavailable for `.app`, `.ai` and `.io`, so a lapsed name of those extensions needs a manual route (control panel or support; not verified), and the Sleeping state must not offer a one-click restore for them (4.3b).
### 4.2 Unit economics

**Finding.** The USD 1.50 flat fee in the brief does not survive contact with Stripe, refunds, disputes and renewal cycles. At OpenSRS Essential wholesale, the fee that gives zero expected contribution on a first order is USD 1.25 for `.com` and `.dev`, 1.50 for `.app`, 2.75 for `.studio`, 4.75 for `.io` and 8.25 for `.ai`. So USD 1.50 sits at or just above break-even on `.com`, `.dev` and `.app` and earns only USD 0.09 to 0.30 per domain-year there (model run at a fee of USD 1.50; `.app` renewals are already slightly negative, at -0.09), and it loses USD 1.04 on `.studio`, USD 2.99 on `.io` and USD 6.02 on `.ai`. A single flat fee also has a structural problem: Stripe's 2.9% scales with the ticket, so the fee that is right for a USD 15 name under-recovers on a `.ai` order (at the recommended fee the order is USD 242 and Stripe alone takes USD 7.32 of it on a domestic card). The model, its inputs and its outputs are in `docs/research/unit-economics.py` and `docs/research/unit-economics-output.md`; it is an analysis script, not application code, and every number in this section comes from that output (regenerated after the review pass). No second agent audited its assumptions; a reviewer's re-run reproduced the output exactly.

**Verified inputs** (all sources accessed 2026-09-29).

| Input | Value | Source |
|---|---|---|
| Stripe card fee | 2.9% + USD 0.30 domestic; +1.5% international card; +1% only if currency conversion is needed; Apple Pay, Google Pay and Link cards the same; hosted Checkout adds no fee | https://stripe.com/pricing ; verified by re-fetch |
| Refund | Free to issue; the original fee is not returned | https://stripe.com/pricing (FAQ) |
| Dispute | USD 15 when received (never returned) plus USD 15 if countered (returned only on a win); a lost, countered dispute on a USD 10 order costs USD 30.59 in fees and reverses the USD 10 | https://stripe.com/pricing |
| Radar | Lite included; Standard USD 0.05 per screened transaction | https://stripe.com/radar/pricing |
| Stripe Tax | 0.5% per transaction through Checkout where registered, or USD 0.50 through the API; break-even USD 100 tax-inclusive | https://stripe.com/tax/pricing ; verifier correction |
| Card programs | Visa VAMP excessive ratio 1.5% in the US since 2026-04-01 with a 1,500-event floor; the Non-compliant tier at 5 events and 0.5% is reachable at launch volume and may carry fees; Stripe's own review is the practical constraint (0.75% "excessive" line) | https://docs.stripe.com/disputes/monitoring-programs ; https://docs.stripe.com/disputes/measuring ; Visa fact sheet https://corporate.visa.com/content/dam/VCOM/corporate/visa-perspectives/security-and-trust/documents/visa-acquirer-monitoring-program-fact-sheet-2025.pdf |
| Wholesale | OpenSRS Essential: `.com` 15.25 (from 2026-11-01), `.dev` 17, `.app` 21, `.studio` 51 (from 2026-10-06), `.io` 60, `.ai` 111 with a 2-year minimum (222 at first charge, inferred from 2 x 111: `get_price` has not been run). The rate card does not say whether the USD 0.20 ICANN fee is included (only `get_price` does). Promotional first-year prices on the same card (`.dev` 10, `.io` 34, `.app` 14) are excluded; whether they apply to API orders is unknown. The Dynadot route in the model uses renewal prices, because the reseller-tab registration prices for `.dev` and `.app` are higher and unexplained | https://opensrs.com/domains/pricing ; https://opensrs.com/domains/tld-price-changes ; https://www.dynadot.com/domain/prices |
| Funding | Card or PayPal deposits at OpenSRS carry a 3% fee that is a gross-up (deposit / 0.97, about +3.09%); ACH is US and Canada only and needs a signed application that Tucows assesses | https://opensrs.com/payment-terms |
| Payouts | First payout typically 7 to 14 days; payout delays are most likely on new accounts; risk-based reserves possible, no numeric policy published | https://docs.stripe.com/payouts ; https://support.stripe.com/questions/payout-availability-delays ; https://support.stripe.com/questions/reserves-frequently-asked-questions |

Effective Stripe cost on a domestic card order: USD 15 costs USD 0.74 (4.9%), USD 20 USD 0.88 (4.4%), USD 25 USD 1.03 (4.1%), USD 60 USD 2.04 (3.4%), USD 115 USD 3.64 (3.2%), USD 226 USD 6.85 (3.0%). International cards add 1.5 points. All money is computed in integer cents or `Decimal`: binary floating point stores 15 x 0.029 + 0.30 as 0.73499999999999998... and rounds it to 0.73, where Stripe charges USD 0.74.

**Assumptions in the model (own assumptions: no published base rate exists; every one is labelled in the script and can be changed).** International cards 15% of orders; refunds 3% of first orders and 0.5% of renewals; failed registrations 1% (authorization voided, no Stripe fee); disputes 0.5% of first orders and 1.0% of renewals with 70% lost after countering; upstream recovery of the wholesale cost on a cancelled gTLD registration 90% (add-grace refund, capped by the AGP Limits Policy, https://www.icann.org/resources/pages/agp-policy-2008-12-17-en), 0% for `.io` and `.ai`; renewals are final upstream (0% recovery); support cost USD 1.50 per refund, USD 5 per dispute, USD 2 per failed registration; no sales tax registrations at launch; wholesale funded by ACH or wire, so no funding fee in the base case. Published digital-goods dispute rates from vendors range from 0.26% to 3.62% with no dataset behind them, so the dispute rate is the softest input, and the base case is not a comfortable one: the base renewal dispute rate of 1.0% is above Stripe's 0.75% line, and the first-order 0.5% equals Visa's Non-compliant ratio. Transfers-in are priced like first orders and not modelled separately: a failed transfer is refunded while Stripe keeps its fee, and no failure rate exists, so that cost is an unquantified gap. Radar Lite is assumed; Radar Standard would cost USD 0.05 per screened order (USD 0.05 per domain-year, about 0.03 for `.ai`).

**Expected contribution per domain-year (USD), first order, OpenSRS Essential, by flat fee F** (the output also has F = 5 and 12).

| Extension | Years per order | Wholesale per year | F = 2 | F = 3 | F = 4 | F = 6 | F = 8 | F = 10 |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| `.com` | 1 | 15.25 | 0.77 | 1.69 | 2.62 | 4.47 | 6.32 | 8.17 |
| `.dev` | 1 | 17.00 | 0.70 | 1.63 | 2.55 | 4.40 | 6.25 | 8.10 |
| `.app` | 1 | 21.00 | 0.55 | 1.48 | 2.40 | 4.25 | 6.10 | 7.95 |
| `.studio` | 1 | 51.00 | -0.57 | 0.35 | 1.28 | 3.13 | 4.98 | 6.83 |
| `.io` | 1 | 60.00 | -2.53 | -1.60 | -0.68 | 1.17 | 3.02 | 4.87 |
| `.ai` | 2 | 111.00 | -5.56 | -4.63 | -3.71 | -1.86 | -0.01 | 1.85 |

**Flat fee needed for a target expected contribution per domain-year** (first order / renewal):

| Target | `.com` | `.dev` | `.app` | `.studio` | `.io` | `.ai` |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| Zero | 1.25 / 1.50 | 1.25 / 1.50 | 1.50 / 1.75 | 2.75 / 3.00 | 4.75 / 3.50 | 8.25 / 5.50 |
| USD 1.50 | 3.00 / 3.00 | 3.00 / 3.00 | 3.25 / 3.25 | 4.25 / 4.75 | 6.50 / 5.00 | 9.75 / 7.00 |
| USD 2.50 | 4.00 / 4.00 | 4.00 / 4.25 | 4.25 / 4.25 | 5.50 / 5.75 | 7.50 / 6.00 | 10.75 / 8.00 |

The same run on Dynadot's list prices needs USD 0 to 2.00 less on each extension (most on `.ai`) but does not change the shape.

**Sensitivity** (fee USD 4 for `.com` and `.app`, USD 9 for `.studio` and `.io`, USD 10 for `.ai`; USD per domain-year; the base case assumes wholesale funded by ACH or wire).

| Case | `.com` | `.app` | `.studio` | `.io` | `.ai` |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| Base case | 2.62 | 2.40 | 5.91 | 3.95 | 1.85 |
| Disputes 0.25% (first) / 0.5% (renewal) | 2.73 | 2.52 | 6.09 | 4.15 | 2.10 |
| Disputes 1.0% / 2.0% | 2.40 | 2.16 | 5.54 | 3.56 | 1.35 |
| Disputes 2.0% / 3.0% (well above Stripe's line; its risk review would intervene) | 1.96 | 1.68 | 4.82 | 2.77 | 0.35 |
| Refunds 8% of first orders | 2.27 | 2.02 | 5.13 | 0.42 | -4.24 |
| All cards international | 2.37 | 2.09 | 5.15 | 3.08 | 0.32 |
| Stripe Tax on every order | 2.52 | 2.28 | 5.62 | 3.62 | 1.27 |
| No upstream refund on cancel (AGP pool exhausted) | 2.21 | 1.83 | 4.53 | 3.95 | 1.85 |
| Wholesale deposits funded by card or PayPal (+3.09% of wholesale) | 2.15 | 1.76 | 4.34 | 2.11 | -1.55 |

A single lost dispute costs the wholesale price plus fees plus USD 35 of dispute fees and support time: about USD 51 on a `.com` order against its USD 2.62 of contribution, and about USD 264 on a `.ai` order against USD 3.70. So the plan treats dispute prevention (clear descriptor, receipts, refund policy, Radar, 3-D Secure on early orders for liability shift on fraud disputes, passkey-approved renewals) as a margin control and not only a risk control. `.io` and `.ai` refunds are unrecoverable upstream, which is why they collapse at 8% refunds, and card funding of the OpenSRS balance costs 0.47 to 3.40 per domain-year (`.com` to `.ai`), which is why ACH or wire approval matters (see "How renewals are charged").

**Recommended fee structure (decision D-003; status: needs your decision, because it departs from the brief's single flat fee: three levels).**
- **Three levels, by the extension's standard wholesale: USD 4.00 per domain-year under USD 50 a year (`.com`, `.dev`, `.app`); USD 9.00 from USD 50 to under USD 100 (`.studio` after 2026-10-06, `.io`); USD 10.00 from USD 100 (`.ai`).** The same fee applies to registration, renewal and transfer-in. Restores pass through the published upstream restore fee plus the same fee. WHOIS privacy is not a line item. A wholesale move across USD 50 or USD 100 moves an extension to the next level, announced under the notice policy below.
- Why three: the test is USD 1.50 of expected contribution per domain-year on a first order, which needs 3.00 (`.com`, `.dev`), 3.25 (`.app`), 4.25 (`.studio`), 6.50 (`.io`) and 9.75 (`.ai`). USD 9.00 clears it for `.studio` and `.io` but not for `.ai` (0.92), and USD 10.00 does (1.85). `.ai` stays thin under stress (0.35 with disputes at 2.0% and 3.0%, -4.24 at 8% refunds) because its refunds are unrecoverable upstream: one more reason it stays gated (D-006). `.studio` and `.io` are over-recovered by design, because each level is one threshold.

| Extension | Wholesale a year, November 2026 | Fee | First-order price | Contribution per domain-year: first order | Contribution per domain-year: renewal |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| `.com` | 15.25 | 4.00 | 19.25 | 2.62 | 2.55 |
| `.dev` | 17.00 | 4.00 | 21.00 | 2.55 | 2.48 |
| `.app` | 21.00 | 4.00 | 25.00 | 2.40 | 2.31 |
| `.studio` | 51.00 | 9.00 | 60.00 | 5.91 | 5.79 |
| `.io` | 60.00 | 9.00 | 69.00 | 3.95 | 5.40 |
| `.ai` | 111.00 (2-year minimum) | 10.00 | 242.00 for two years (121.00 a year) | 1.85 | 4.46 (8.92 per two years) |

- The Hatch sheet shows "Registry and registrar cost + Mosshatch fee = price" and "Renews at" at the same size. For `.ai` the first row reads "First 2 years (minimum)" because the registry allows no shorter term. The renewal line reads, for example, "USD 19.25 the first year, then USD 19.25 a year. Renewal prices can change; we email you 21 days before any charge", not "renews the same", because wholesale can change on 30 days' notice. The sheet shows the amount OpenSRS actually charged: if promotional first-year prices apply to API orders (unknown, question 12) the sheet shows the real amount or the promotion is declined, so that the breakdown is true.
- **Mosshatch will not be the cheapest.** Public retail renewals on the day of research were `.com` 10.46 to 11.08, `.studio` 31.20 to 33.39, `.io` 50.00 to 53.50 and `.ai` 80.00 to 85.60 at Cloudflare, Porkbun and Dynadot (sources, accessed 2026-09-29: https://cfdomainpricing.com/prices.json, a third-party mirror of Cloudflare's prices; https://api.porkbun.com/api/json/v3/pricing/get ; https://www.dynadot.com/domain/prices). The positioning has to rest on the honest, itemized price and the product, not on price. Two levers exist: OpenSRS volume tiers (Advanced needs USD 2,000 of spend and 100 registrations a year and takes USD 1 off `.com`; Enterprise USD 3 to 12 off) and per-extension routing to a cheaper provider once the adapter layer is proven (for example Dynadot's `.studio` at 33.39).
- Alternatives considered: one flat fee of USD 4 (sell `.com`, `.dev`, `.app` only; `.studio` earns 1.28, `.io` loses 0.68, `.ai` loses 3.71); one flat fee of USD 9 for everything (`.com` becomes USD 24.25, uncompetitive, and `.ai` earns only 0.92); a percentage fee (rejected: it is not a flat fee and hides the card cost).
- `.ai` should not go on sale until the OpenSRS `.ai` path has been tested (not available in Horizon), the two-year and two-year-transfer charges are confirmed with `get_price`, and the public-contact exposure (no privacy service) is disclosed at the point of sale.

**Premium names are not sold at launch (D-031).** (a) The OpenSRS account leaves the registry-premium categories disabled (they are opt-in per account, MSA 10.6), so premium names come back from the registrar as unavailable. The UI labels them "not available", never "taken", and `Availability.kind = premium` renders the same way. Aftermarket premium names (MSA 10.2 to 10.4) are not enabled either. (b) A price guard runs on every quote (register, renew, transfer-in): the fee level is set by the extension's standard wholesale, not by a per-name quote, and any quote that is registry-premium (`is_registry_premium`) or differs from the extension's standard price is refused before payment with a plain message. That covers transfer-in and renewal of an already-registered premium-priced name: Vercel's registrar price endpoint showed a registered `harbor.app` renewing at USD 108.90 against a standard USD 15 at that registrar on 2026-09-29 (`docs/research/availability-at-scale.verify-skeptic.md`), while OpenSRS's standard `.app` is USD 21. A Phase 2 test feeds a mock quote at a non-standard price and requires the order to be refused. (c) Reasons: premium transactions are non-refundable upstream (10.6), which breaks the refund path the model assumes; a premium renewal needs an explicit request that echoes the quoted price (10.7, Appendix A); premium data may not be copied, stored or archived (10.9), unlike the availability cache in 4.3b; premium fees can change without notice (Appendix A); and the model gives USD -1.50 per domain-year for a USD 108.90 renewal at the USD 4 fee. (d) Selling premium names later needs its own decision: a per-name fee rule, a no-refund disclosure and the renewal price shown before payment. Source, accessed 2026-09-29: https://assets.opensrs.com/Uploads/Master-Services-Agreement.pdf.

**How renewals are charged.**
1. Auto-renew is opt-in, never pre-checked, and is a passkey-signed mandate with a price ceiling (compliance details in section 4.7).
2. The charge is an off-session PaymentIntent on the saved card at charge date C, ten days before expiry E (C = E-10). The decline ladder runs at C, C+3 and C+6, leaving at least seven days to pay by other means. Card authentication failures (`authentication_required`) email an on-session pay link.
3. **Charge first, renew upstream second.** Renewals are final at OpenSRS ("cannot be cancelled under any circumstances"), so the upstream call happens only after the charge succeeds, out of a prepaid balance sized under "Working capital" below. The base case assumes no funding fee, because OpenSRS charges none for ACH or wire (the sender's bank may). ACH is not automatic: it needs a signed contract that Tucows assesses and either authorizes or declines (three business days on the payment-terms page, typically 3 to 5 in the support articles), then carries a per-payment ceiling and a cap on requests in any three-day window, and a second failed attempt costs USD 1 and a final failure USD 25. Apply when the account is opened, before launch. Until approved, or if declined, fund by wire, or by card or PayPal at +3.09% of wholesale (the last sensitivity row). Sources, accessed 2026-09-29: https://opensrs.com/payment-terms ; https://support.opensrs.com/support/solutions/articles/201000063400.
4. Wholesale price changes pass through. Registry increases are announced months ahead (`.com` 6 months), but OpenSRS promises only at least 30 days for its own price changes (Appendix A; pass-through fee changes under MSA 12.6 and premium-name fees carry no such promise), and when the two scheduled rises were first posted is unknown. So the renewal quote shown in the panel is refreshed daily, and the notice policy below applies.

**Renewal-notice policy (stated here once; 4.1 and COMPLIANCE C-33 refer to it).** The charge is at C = E-10 days. Notices go out at E-43, E-32, C-8 (= E-18) and E+1, and the ICANN expiry notices are sent regardless. A price-change reminder is emailed 21 days before the charge, inside California's 7 to 30 day window and New York's window (`docs/research/lifecycle-and-consumer-law.md`). A change to Mosshatch's own flat fee is announced at least 60 days ahead as an informational notice and is still repeated 21 days before the first affected charge. A wholesale change that affects a renewal is emailed as soon as it is known and repeated 21 days before the charge. The flat fee is fixed for a term already quoted.

**Fixed costs (infrastructure only; excludes labour, counsel, accounting, insurance and any Stripe Tax Complete plan at USD 90 a month).** Recommended path (D-005 Static IPs, D-014 Neon Scale, Resend Pro): Vercel Pro 20 + Static IPs 100 + Neon Scale 42 to 45 + Resend Pro 20 + AWS KMS 4 + domains and monitoring 15 = about USD 201 to 204 a month, plus Private Data Transfer, which is not estimated (from USD 0.15 per GB, for all outbound traffic of the registrar project including builds). Gateway path (the D-005 alternative, still on Neon Scale, with Resend Free): 20 + 42 to 45 + 4 + 6 + 15 = about USD 87 to 90 a month.

| Path | Fixed cost per month (USD) | Domain-years a month to break even at USD 1.50 / 2.50 / 3.00 per domain-year |
|---|---|---|
| Recommended | 201 to 204 | 134 to 136 / 81 to 82 / 67 to 68 |
| Gateway | 87 to 90 | 58 to 60 / 35 to 36 / 29 to 30 |

The Neon figure is Scale compute for an always-on 0.25 CU (187.5 CU-hours x USD 0.222 = USD 41.63; scale-to-zero off in production, as the Neon dossier recommends) plus storage (USD 0.35 per GB-month) and restore history (USD 0.20 per GB-month). The KMS figure is four keys at USD 1 (two in production, one each in staging and development, D-026), about USD 12 a month by year three with rotation. The Neon, gateway and monitoring figures are our own estimates. Before real customer money (Phases 1 to 3: Neon Launch at about USD 20 of compute, Resend Free, no Static IPs because Horizon needs none) the running cost is about USD 59 a month (own arithmetic: 20 + 19.88 + 4 + 15). USD 2.50 is roughly what the USD 4 level earns on `.com`, `.dev` and `.app`. The USD 95 OpenSRS activation fee is non-refundable but converts to account credit (plus GST or HST where applicable), so it is prepaid wholesale and not a running cost. Not costed: a Vercel log drain and Observability, mailboxes, a status page hosted off Vercel and AWS, the independent security review (D-032), counsel, accounting and insurance. Sources, accessed 2026-09-29: https://vercel.com/docs/networking/static-ips ; https://vercel.com/pricing ; https://neon.com/docs/introduction/plans ; https://resend.com/pricing ; https://aws.amazon.com/kms/pricing/ ; https://opensrs.com/payment-terms.

**Working capital.** This, not the USD 95 activation fee, is the largest cash requirement before launch. Registrations spend wholesale from the OpenSRS balance the moment they succeed, while Stripe pays out 7 to 14 days after the first charges and may hold reserves; refunds also need available Stripe balance ("Stripe holds the refund as pending" when it is short, https://docs.stripe.com/refunds.md); and a balance below zero at OpenSRS is a material breach (MSA 12.1) while a depleted one blocks all services (MSA 12.7). Required float F = (registrations per day at the daily cap x average wholesale x 14 days of payout lag) + (wholesale of renewals due in the next 45 days, zero in the first year) + (USD 222 for each `.ai` order the cap allows inside the lag, once `.ai` is on sale) + (5% of the last 30 days of captures as a refund buffer). Illustration, own arithmetic and own targets: a launch cap of 5 registrations a day at an average wholesale of USD 17 gives 5 x 17 x 14 = USD 1,190, so hold about USD 1,200 plus the refund buffer. Alert at 50% of F (own target). The cap is a database limit (`limits.daily_registrations`), so exposure is bounded, and new registrations are refused when the available balance minus reserved wholesale falls below USD 250 (own target; renewals already charged come first; see 4.3b). ACH with a balance trigger is the standing top-up; card top-ups (+3.09%, up to two business days to apply) are the emergency channel, because ACH cannot refill inside a weekend. Enrol in ACH before Phase 6 and allow about a week. You decide the cash you put in; the proposal is the cap of 5 a day and USD 1,200 of float until 30 days of clean reconciliation.

**Cost controls.** Most providers offer alerts, not caps that suit a registrar, and a paused production is worse than an overspend, so each control below is an alert plus a human decision unless stated. Figures marked own target are ours.

| Service | Setting | Basis (accessed 2026-09-29) |
|---|---|---|
| Vercel | Spend Management: an on-demand budget of USD 100 (own target; new teams start at USD 200), notifications at 50, 75 and 100%, SMS at 100%, and a webhook to the alert channel. Leave "Pause Production Deployments" off | "Setting a spend amount does not stop usage on its own"; checks are periodic, so alerts can arrive minutes late; separate add-ons such as Static IPs are outside the amount. https://vercel.com/docs/spend-management |
| Neon | Spending notifications; autoscaling maximum 2 CU on production (own target); scale-to-zero off on the production branch only; preview branch time-to-live of 1 day (own target); a per-project quota only on non-production projects | Notifications fire at 80% and 100% of a threshold and no hard cap is documented. https://neon.com/docs/changelog ; https://neon.com/docs/introduction/plans |
| Resend | Pro before the first customer; alerts at a 2% bounce rate, a 0.04% spam rate and 80% of the daily quota (own targets) | Overage stops at 5x the monthly quota and sending then pauses until the next cycle; sending may also pause above a 4% bounce rate or a 0.08% spam rate. https://resend.com/docs/knowledge-base/account-quotas-and-limits |
| AWS | AWS Budgets at USD 20 a month (own target) and an alarm on KMS request volume | Keys cost USD 1 per key-month; the first and second rotation add USD 1 each. https://aws.amazon.com/kms/pricing/ |
| OpenSRS | A daily lookup ceiling with the circuit breaker (4.6, availability abuse); the float alert at 50% of F | The "excessive use" lookup fee is unquantified (MSA 3.2), so the ceiling is agreed in writing (question 1) |
| Stripe | Alert when the dispute ratio reaches 0.5% (own target, equal to Visa's Non-compliant ratio) | https://docs.stripe.com/disputes/monitoring-programs |

### 4.3 Architecture

Stack verdicts first (4.3a), then the architecture that follows from them (4.3b).
### 4.3a Stack verdicts (confirm or challenge)

Versions are the `latest` npm tags on 2026-09-29 unless stated (each package's page is `https://registry.npmjs.org/<package>`, accessed 2026-09-29). Load-bearing external facts carry their primary URL and the access date 2026-09-29; every entry is also backed by `docs/research/tech-*.md` and its verification notes. Customer environments are called project environments (`dev`, `preview`, `prod`) in this plan; Mosshatch's own environments are called deployment environments (local, preview, staging, production; see 4.3b).

| Brief choice | Verdict | What the research says and what changes |
|---|---|---|
| Vite + React + TypeScript | **Confirm, with a toolchain note** | Vite 8.3.1 (Rolldown and Oxc; there is no Rollup or esbuild in the default build; https://vite.dev/releases, accessed 2026-09-29), `@vitejs/plugin-react` 6.1.1 (peer `vite ^8`), React 19.3.0, TypeScript 7.0.2 (native compiler, no stable JS API; https://devblogs.microsoft.com/typescript/announcing-typescript-7-0/, accessed 2026-09-29). `typescript-eslint` peers `typescript <6.1.0`, so install TS through Microsoft's documented alias (`typescript` to `@typescript/typescript6`, `@typescript/native` to `typescript@^7`). Tested on the recommended manifest (260 packages, 0 audit findings): `tsc` 7 and 6, Vite build, Vitest 5, ESLint 10, `drizzle-kit generate` and size-limit all pass on Node 24.21 and 26.10. A separate install of all 35 requested packages at `latest` resolves with no peer conflicts (494 packages) but has 9 vulnerable packages from `@vercel/node` and `drizzle-kit`'s `@esbuild-kit` esbuild. That manifest predates D-014 (`pg`) and D-021 (Playwright 1.56.1), so it is re-installed and re-audited at the start of Phase 1 |
| three.js used imperatively, WebGL2 | **Confirm** | three 0.186.1 (ESM-only, cadence slowed to about 70 days; https://registry.npmjs.org/three). WebGL2 is 96.4% of global usage (caniuse-db 2026-09-28, https://raw.githubusercontent.com/Fyrd/caniuse/main/features-json/webgl2.json, accessed 2026-09-29; floors iOS Safari 15, Chrome Android 58). `WebGPURenderer` is still experimental and does not support custom `ShaderMaterial` (https://threejs.org/manual/pages/webgpurenderer.html, accessed 2026-09-29), so it cannot be the sole target. Watch: `Clock` is deprecated (use `Timer`); `ColorManagement` on by default; `ShaderMaterial` must include `colorspace_fragment` itself. three does not declare `sideEffects: false` and a bare `WebGLRenderer` is 128 kB gzip, so tree shaking helps little: budget for a lazily loaded scene chunk |
| Zustand as bridge | **Confirm** | 5.0.15, declares `sideEffects: false`, about 0.6 kB |
| Vercel functions | **Confirm, on Pro, as three projects** | Hobby is contractually personal and non-commercial and its definition of commercial includes "requesting or processing payment from visitors" (https://vercel.com/docs/limits/fair-use-guidelines, accessed 2026-09-29): Pro is required (USD 20 per month with one deploying seat and USD 20 usage credit; https://vercel.com/pricing, accessed 2026-09-29). One Pro team runs all three projects (`web`, `registrar`, `cards`; 4.3b Deployment topology). Use Node.js Fluid functions, Node 24.x (Node 20 is disabled for new deployments on 2026-10-01; https://vercel.com/changelog/node-js-20-is-being-deprecated, accessed 2026-09-29), not Edge. Limits on Pro: 300 s default and 800 s maximum duration, 4.5 MB body (https://vercel.com/docs/functions/limitations, accessed 2026-09-29). No SLA on Pro (Enterprise 99.99%; https://vercel.com/legal/sla, accessed 2026-09-29). The status history shows 133 incidents in the last 12 months (3 critical, 28 major, 81 minor; https://www.vercel-status.com/history, accessed 2026-09-29); the iad1 incidents that matter here are 2025-10-20 and 21 (major, multi-service, Cron Jobs affected) and 2026-05-08 (classed minor, about five hours of elevated function failures, Queues and Workflow affected). Runtime logs are kept 1 day on Pro (https://vercel.com/docs/logs/runtime, accessed 2026-09-29), so audit evidence must live in the database, not in Vercel logs. Fixed egress for the registrar is Vercel Static IPs: USD 100 per project per month plus Private Data Transfer from USD 0.15 per GB for all outbound traffic of that project including builds, one address pair per region shared with a small group of other customers (https://vercel.com/docs/networking/static-ips, last updated 2026-06-30, accessed 2026-09-29; 4.3b Egress) |
| Durable `jobs` table plus cron | **Confirm** | Cron on Pro can run every minute (100 jobs per project, UTC, production only, `Authorization: Bearer $CRON_SECRET`) but delivery is "best effort": it can be duplicated or silently missed and failed runs are not retried (https://vercel.com/docs/cron-jobs/usage-and-pricing, accessed 2026-09-29). A Postgres table claimed with `FOR UPDATE SKIP LOCKED` plus a one-minute sweeper matches Vercel's own guidance, and a heartbeat checked from outside covers a tick that never runs (4.3b Jobs). Vercel Queues is beta and the Workflow SDK has no GA announcement: keep them off the money path |
| Neon Postgres with Drizzle | **Confirm, with a driver change** | Drizzle's `neon-http` driver still has no interactive transactions (`db.transaction()` throws in drizzle-orm 0.45.3 and 1.0.0-rc.4), which the order ledger and audit writes need. Neon recommends `node-postgres` over TCP with a pool and `attachDatabasePool` on Vercel Fluid (https://neon.com/docs/connect/choose-connection, https://orm.drizzle.team/docs/connect-neon, accessed 2026-09-29): use that (or the WebSocket `neon-serverless` driver). Region: Neon `aws-us-east-1` pairs with Vercel `iad1`, and a Neon region can never be changed (https://neon.com/docs/introduction/regions, accessed 2026-09-29). Create the runtime role with SQL: roles made in the Console, API or CLI (including `neondb_owner`) are `neon_superuser` with BYPASSRLS and can write any table regardless of REVOKEs (https://neon.com/docs/manage/roles, accessed 2026-09-29). `drizzle-orm` must be at least 0.45.2 (CVE-2026-39356, SQL injection through dynamic identifiers; https://raw.githubusercontent.com/drizzle-team/drizzle-orm/main/changelogs/drizzle-orm/0.45.2.md, accessed 2026-09-29). The Drizzle 0.45 migrator skips out-of-order migrations, so migration files need monotonic timestamps. Plans: Launch (usage-based, PITR 7 days, billing support only) is fine for Phases 1 to 3; the 30-day PITR, IP allow-list and SOC 2 report access are Scale features, so plan Scale before real customer money (https://neon.com/docs/introduction/plans, accessed 2026-09-29). A child branch inherits the parent's data, including registrant PII and vault ciphertext, so previews branch from a seeded, anonymised branch and never from production (4.3b Environments) |
| SimpleWebAuthn | **Confirm** | `@simplewebauthn/server` 14.0.3, `/browser` 14.0.0, Node 22 or later. WebAuthn Level 3 became a W3C Recommendation on 2026-08-25 (https://api.w3.org/specifications/webauthn-3/versions/latest, accessed 2026-09-29). The library is stateless: a valid assertion verified twice in the research harness passed both times at counter 0, so replay defence is ours (single-use challenges). Set `userVerification: 'required'` explicitly (the library default is `'preferred'`) and pin `supportedAlgorithmIDs` to `[-7, -257]` at registration, because 14.x prepends ML-DSA-44 and throws `PQCNotSupportedError` otherwise |
| Stripe Checkout (hosted) | **Confirm; change the payment gate** | stripe-node 22.6.2 pins API `2026-08-26.dahlia`: pin the package (`~22.6.2`) and pass `apiVersion` explicitly, and set it on the webhook endpoint (https://docs.stripe.com/api/versioning.md, accessed 2026-09-29). See D-002: authorize, register, then capture, instead of registering after `payment_intent.succeeded` (https://docs.stripe.com/payments/place-a-hold-on-a-payment-method.md, accessed 2026-09-29) |
| Resend | **Confirm** | Free 3,000 per month and 100 per day; Pro USD 20 for 50,000; 10 requests per second; `Idempotency-Key` on send (24 h).
